<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog post is the third part of a blog series called <a href="https://nam06.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fazure.microsoft.com%2Fen-us%2Fblog%2Ftag%2Fazure-iaas%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7Cv-estvirko%40microsoft.com%7C6e1c78a85c2040ce3c1108ded1fb3397%7C72f988bf86f141af91ab2d7cd011db47%7C1%7C0%7C639179074098601042%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=mnBTkxRVox%2F%2FXUsLjCQqgid0goRkY3S7s7WFEnDINaI%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS</a> which will share best practices and guidance to help you build a trusted infrastructure platform—from performance, resiliency, and security to scalability and cost efficiency.</em></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations modernize infrastructure, migrate mission-critical workloads, build cloud-native applications, and scale AI—<a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/cloud-cost-optimization-principles-that-still-matter/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">cost efficiency remains a foundational principle</a> of cloud architectures.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://aka.ms/azureIaaS" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discover the Benefits of Azure IaaS</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Yet cloud costs are rarely driven by a single decision. More often, across Azure Infrastructure-as-a-Service (IaaS) environments, they are the result of many compounded architectural choices across compute, storage, and networking. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Common examples include overprovisioning infrastructure when selecting a larger virtual machine than a workload requires or keeping infrequently accessed data on premium storage, building resilient architectures that introduce unnecessary overhead, or collecting and retaining more operational data than is needed. Individually, these decisions may seem minor, but over time they can significantly impact both cost and operational efficiency. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These challenges become even more important as organizations expand AI initiatives, modernize applications, and support growing performance and resiliency requirements. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The opportunity lies in addressing these inefficiencies before they become entrenched. By making informed infrastructure decisions during planning, deployment, and ongoing operations, organizations can improve resource utilization, reduce total cost of ownership (TCO), and create a more scalable foundation for future growth.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In this blog, we&#8217;ll explore some of the most common infrastructure cost challenges organizations face today and examine how Azure IaaS capabilities across compute, storage, and networking can help improve efficiency, reduce TCO, and highlight resources available in the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS Resource Center</a> to help you make more informed decisions.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Many of the most impactful optimization opportunities originate long before a workload enters production. To better understand where these opportunities exist, let&#8217;s examine common efficiency challenges (and solutions) across compute, storage, and networking.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="compute-matching-resources-to-workload-requirements"><a id="post-52339-_Toc598210129"></a>Compute: Matching resources to workload requirements</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Compute inefficiencies are often the easiest to identify because they directly affect both performance and infrastructure spend.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The goal is not simply to select the lowest-cost compute option, but rather to align infrastructure resources with workload requirements while preserving flexibility for future growth.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machines">Azure provides a broad portfolio of virtual machine options</a>, enabling organizations to select the architecture, processor type, performance profile, and scale characteristics that best match workload needs; allowing organizations to align infrastructure investments with workload needs rather than paying for unused capacity. 
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Equally important is taking advantage of <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing">Azure&#8217;s flexible pricing options</a>. Depending on workload characteristics, organizations can combine Pay-As-You-Go pricing, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/savings-plans">Azure savings plans</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cost-management-billing/reservations/save-compute-costs-reservations">Azure Reservations</a>, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machines/spot">Azure Spot Virtual Machines</a> to better align costs with actual usage patterns.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  For highly scalable environments, services such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machine-scale-sets">Azure Virtual Machine Scale Sets</a> automatically balance compute demand with available capacity by scaling resources up or down in real time, ensuring the environment is right-sized while optimizing cost efficiency. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/compute-fleet">Azure Compute Fleet</a> help organizations intelligently balance capacity, availability, and price-performance across large deployments; reducing the operational complexity associated with managing infrastructure at scale.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The result is a compute environment that is not only cost-efficient, but also better aligned to application requirements and business outcomes.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="storage-balancing-performance-and-lifecycle-management"><a id="post-52339-_Toc646919671"></a>Storage: Balancing performance and lifecycle management</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Storage inefficiencies often develop gradually, at times making them difficult to identify until environments reach significant scale. The key is to ensure that performance, capacity, and data access requirements remain aligned.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="choose-the-right-storage-service-for-the-workload">Choose the right storage service for the workload</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Storage performance requirements vary dramatically across workloads. Some applications demand consistent low-latency block storage, while others prioritize storage capacity, durability, or long-term retention. Selecting the appropriate storage service and performance tier is critical to maximizing both efficiency and value.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  For example:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Business applications may benefit from <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-gb/products/storage/disks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Premium SSD v2 offerings</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Business-critical transactional databases may require <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-gb/products/storage/disks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Ultra Disk</a> to meet stringent low-latency performance requirements.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Large-scale block storage environments can benefit from consolidated architectures using <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/elastic-san">Azure Elastic Storage Area Network (SAN)</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Linux/Windows file shares, home directories, and shared storage scenarios may benefit from <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/files">Azure Files</a> or <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/netapp">Azure NetApp Files</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Object storage workloads often benefit from the alignment between <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/blobs">Azure Blob Storage</a> tiers and data access patterns.</li>
</ul>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="automate-data-lifecycle-management">Automate data lifecycle management</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Equally important is ensuring data remains on the appropriate storage tier throughout its lifecycle. In many environments, data access patterns change significantly over time, yet storage configurations remain static. This disconnect often results in organizations paying for performance they no longer need.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/blobs">Azure Blob Storage</a> provides capabilities that help organizations automatically align storage costs with data access patterns. Automated tiering and lifecycle policies maintain low-latency access for frequently used data while optimizing costs by transitioning infrequently accessed data to lower-cost tiers.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The result is a storage strategy that continuously adapts as usage patterns evolve, without requiring ongoing manual intervention.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="improve-visibility-across-your-storage-estate">Improve visibility across your storage estate</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Optimization starts with understanding where costs are being generated.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Tools such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage-discovery">Azure Storage Discovery</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage-actions">Azure Storage Actions</a> can help organizations gain visibility into their storage environments, uncover optimization opportunities, and automate actions across large-scale deployments.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Rather than managing storage account by account, teams can identify patterns and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/cloud-cost-optimization-how-to-maximize-roi-from-ai-manage-costs-and-unlock-real-business-value/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">implement cost-saving actions</a> consistently across their entire data estate.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities help organizations move beyond storage provisioning and toward ongoing storage optimization.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="networking-improving-efficiency-without-compromising-resiliency"><a id="post-52339-_Toc155015881"></a>Networking: Improving efficiency without compromising resiliency</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Networking presents a unique optimization challenge because organizations must balance connectivity, performance, resiliency, and operational visibility.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="achieve-resiliency-more-efficiently">Achieve resiliency more efficiently</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Historically, improving resiliency often requires duplicating infrastructure components, creating additional cost and management overhead. Today, organizations increasingly seek architectures that deliver resiliency while minimizing complexity and excess infrastructure.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/category/networking">Azure networking capabilities</a> help organizations evaluate these tradeoffs more effectively. Services such as <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/expressroute/metro">ExpressRoute Metro</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-nat-gateway">Zone Redundant NAT Gateway</a>, and scalable networking architectures provide opportunities to improve resiliency and scalability while maintaining operational efficiency.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="reduce-operational-and-logging-expenses">Reduce operational and logging expenses</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Operational visibility is another important consideration. Network and firewall logs are essential for troubleshooting, security, and governance, but collecting every possible data point can create significant storage and operational costs over time.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Modern filtering and analytics capabilities help teams focus on the most relevant network data, reducing both storage consumption and investigation complexity.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This gives organizations the information they need while avoiding excessive log growth and long-term retention costs.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">By implementing filtering, automation, and intelligent logging strategies, organizations can focus on the data that provides actionable insights while reducing unnecessary information collection and retention.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="continuous-optimization-is-where-long-term-savings-happen"><a id="post-52339-_Toc1333105540"></a>Continuous optimization is where long-term savings happen</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Infrastructure efficiency is not achieved through a single migration, architecture review, or pricing decision.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As workloads evolve, usage patterns shift, and new platform capabilities become available, opportunities for optimization continuously emerge.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The organizations that realize the greatest value from cloud investments are often those that treat optimization as an ongoing operational discipline. They regularly evaluate infrastructure utilization, revisit architectural assumptions, automate lifecycle management processes, and adopt new capabilities that improve efficiency across their environments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">While individual improvements may appear incremental, the cumulative impact can be substantial. A right-sized virtual machine (VM), a more appropriate storage tier, an automated lifecycle policy, or a more efficient networking architecture may each deliver modest savings independently. Together, they create a more efficient, scalable, and resilient infrastructure foundation.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Azure continues to deliver important capabilities such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot">Azure Copilot</a> to help customers optimize cloud costs by combining real-time insights, AI-driven recommendations, and automated optimization actions, empowering teams to quickly identify waste, right-size resources, and forecast spend with minimal effort.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="continue-your-azure-iaas-optimization-journey"><a id="post-52339-_Toc561313402"></a>Continue your Azure IaaS optimization journey</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Whether you&#8217;re supporting AI workloads, modernizing existing applications, migrating existing workloads, or planning future growth, building efficiency into cloud architectures has never been more important.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Azure IaaS Resource Center provides guidance, best practices, technical resources, and optimization strategies across compute, storage, and networking to help you design, build, and optimize Azure environments with confidence.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Visit the </strong><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Azure IaaS Resource Center</strong></a> to explore cost optimization guidance, architectural best practices, product resources, and tools that can help you maximize value from your Azure infrastructure investments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To go deeper, explore the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS Resource Center</a> for tutorials, best practices, and guidance across compute, storage, and networking to help you design and operate resilient infrastructure with greater confidence.</p>
